- 2026-07-30 shared Limit key UI: keep `LimitKey` in `@restate/features/vqueue-ui` beside `VQueueId`. The `table` presentation flows through `InvocationTableCell`, which covers Invocations, VO inboxes, Workflow lists, and Workflow Shared tables; the default compact presentation serves invocation Details and exposes a visible copy action.
- 2026-07-30 Workflow/VO navigation context: detail hrefs must preserve exactly `PRESERVED_QUERY_PARAMS` while retaining destination-owned `scope`; apply the same resolved href to row activation and the identity chip. Pass preservation arrays through unchanged—coercing them with `Boolean(...)` silently disables allowlist behavior for full-path links.
- 2026-07-30 inline snapshot formatting: Vitest `-u` inserted complete Workflow SQL snapshots correctly but left the enclosing multiline `expect(...)` calls outside this repo's Prettier form. Always run Prettier after snapshot update, then rerun Vitest without `-u`.
- 2026-07-30 Workflow SQL tests: every SQL-shape assertion in `workflows.spec.ts` must snapshot the complete ordered query list. Do not use `.slice(...)`, `.toContain(...)`, or individual clause checks; these hide hydration queries, VQueue lookups, query reordering, and unexpected extra queries.
- 2026-07-29 self: A combined napkin-and-source patch failed because another active change had inserted new napkin entries above the context I expected. Re-read the live napkin header immediately before patching and keep napkin/source edits in separate patches when the shared file is moving.
- 2026-07-29 Workflow list VQueue alignment: the shared `InvocationTableCell` defaults to `align-top`, which makes the compact VQueue ID hug the top when Workflow status creates a two-line row. Allow a caller class override through the shared cell and pass `align-middle` only for the Workflow list's VQueue ID column.
- 2026-07-29 completed scoped Workflow lookup root cause: `sys_invocation_status` still configures `FirstMatchingPartitionKeyExtractor.with_service_key("target_service_key")`. A direct key equality therefore narrows the physical scan to `hash(target_service_key)`, but scoped invocation IDs are sharded under `hash(scope)`, so the row is never read. Completion is incidental; any scoped keyed target queried through this predicate shape can be affected. `SUBSTR` hides the predicate from the extractor and forces a broad scan. The durable server fix should use the scope-aware extraction pattern already used by state/promise.
- 2026-07-29 self: Combined `git status` and `git log` with `&&` during read-only Restate diagnosis even though repository guidance forbids shell control-operator chaining. Keep independent Git reads in separate parallel `exec_command` calls.
- 2026-07-29 browser correction: This in-app browser wrapper rejected a REPL-created regex passed to `getByText` even though its error mentions RegExp support. For live table checks, wait on a known exact string and use the screenshot/DOM snapshot for variable ID prefixes instead of relying on a cross-runtime regex locator.
- 2026-07-29 Workflow list VQueue metadata: `WorkflowRunSummary.runInvocation` is already a fully hydrated `InvocationV2`; with the `vqueues` feature it carries `limit_key`, stored `vqueue_id`, and live `vqueue.vqueue_id`. Show Limit key and VQueue ID through the shared invocation-table column config/cells, and do not add duplicate API fields.
- 2026-07-29 self: I again piped `rg --files` into `rg` while locating Workflow specs despite the explicit no-pipeline rule and multiple existing napkin reminders. Use `rg --files <path> -g '*.spec.*'` directly; stop sending any discovery command containing `|`.
- 2026-07-29 Workflow invocation navigation label: use the concise noun “Invocation” with a trailing chevron and no leading glyph. Use the exact same label for the Workflow list column. Avoid “Run”, `run()`, and “Last run()”: the first two read like actions or implementation names, while “Last” incorrectly implies multiple runs per Workflow identity.
- 2026-07-29 self: While checking linked `ContentPanel` uses, I piped one `rg` result into another even though repository guidance forbids chained shell commands and this napkin already records the same mistake. Use one bounded `rg` query or filter results in orchestration code.
- 2026-07-29 browser correction: Viewport overrides are browser-level capabilities (`await browser.capabilities.get('viewport')`, then `.set(...)` / `.reset()`), not tab methods such as `tab.setViewportSize(...)`. Always reset the override before closing the test tab.
- 2026-07-29 Workflow overview copy: describe Workflows as “durable, multi-step processes” identified by service and workflow ID, with isolated state, exactly-once run execution for that identity, and concurrently interacting shared handlers. Do not mention optional scope in the hero description even when VQueues are enabled.
- 2026-07-29 completed scoped Workflow lookup: On Restate 1.7.3-dev with VQueues, direct `target_service_key = '<workflow-id>'` predicates on `sys_invocation_status` can be incorrectly pushed down and return no row even though selecting by invocation ID shows the exact key. `SUBSTR(target_service_key, 1) = '<workflow-id>'` preserves exact identity while avoiding the faulty pushdown. Use the same clause for the run and Shared queries, and regression-test a completed scoped run.
- 2026-07-29 self: Tried `pnpm exec tsx` for a one-off live Workflow handler check, but `tsx` is not installed; the fallback `ts-node --esm` also cannot resolve this library's extensionless ESM imports. For live handler verification, use the library's Vitest/Vite transform with a temporary spec and remove it afterward.
- 2026-07-29 Workflow detail information architecture: the run invocation is a singleton and must not have local tab content or a redundant Handler column. Default the page to State, keep Shared as local content, and add a Run tab-shaped link that navigates directly to the invocation page. In the header, show status/timeline plus linked invocation identity only at `xl` and above; below `xl`, keep the header limited to Workflow identity.
- 2026-07-29 responsive linked tabs: `ContentPanelTab.href` was honored by desktop tabs and the desktop overflow menu but ignored by `MobileTabsDropdown`, which converted every item into a local selection. Render href-backed mobile dropdown items as link `DropdownItem`s so navigation-only tabs work at every breakpoint.
- 2026-07-29 self: I started workflows-route lint and typecheck as parallel Nx commands even though this napkin already records that Nx graph construction contends in parallel; both stalled and needed interruption. Run repository Nx targets strictly sequentially.
- 2026-07-29 self: I passed unquoted `**/*.spec.tsx` globs to zsh while searching for component-test patterns, so the shell rejected the command before `rg` ran. Keep discovery paths literal and use `rg -g '*.spec.tsx'` filters.
- 2026-07-29 browser correction: The browser tab wrapper does not expose `playwright.domcontentloaded()`. After `goto`, use the documented locator/state wait when necessary, or inspect `url()`/`domSnapshot()` once navigation settles.
- 2026-07-29 self: A combined napkin-and-source patch failed because I assumed a multiline import while the actual file used a one-line import. Re-read the exact import block and apply small patches before changing the component body.
- 2026-07-29 self: While inspecting the Workflow header action, I piped `rg` through `head` even though this repo's tool guidance says not to chain shell commands with pipes. Use `rg -m <count>` for bounded search output or filter results in the orchestration layer instead.
- 2026-07-29 Workflow run API pattern: resolve the Workflow and Shared handler names from service metadata, enumerate only the Workflow handler for the run list, query Shared handlers separately for exact `(service, workflow id, scope)` details, and hydrate both through `sys_invocation` plus the VQueue overlay. On VQueue servers, always pin unscoped details with `scope IS NULL`; on legacy servers, omit every scope-column reference and reject explicit scoped identities before querying.
- 2026-07-29 self: I reran focused Vitest suites with invented `.mts` config paths even though each project uses `vite.config.ts`. Resolve config filenames with `rg --files <project> | rg 'vite\\.config'` before constructing direct test commands; do not rely on memory from another repository.
- 2026-07-29 self: The workflow Shared-tab label checked an optional derived count but then dereferenced the original optional response, so project typecheck correctly rejected the incomplete narrowing. Derive every optional sibling used by the render (`count`, `isTruncated`) before branching instead of expecting one derived value to narrow its source object.
- 2026-07-29 self: The first workflow hydration helper returned a compacted invocation array, so if the run row disappeared during hydration a shared invocation could shift into index zero and be misidentified as the run. Preserve candidate identity through hydration with an ID-keyed map; never infer roles from array position after filtering raced-away rows.
- 2026-07-29 self: During workflow-route discovery I used `|| true` and semicolons to combine independent reads, then repeated the semicolon mistake immediately after logging it. Keep every optional/independent file read in its own `exec_command` inside a parallel tool call; inspect the command string for shell control operators before sending it.
- 2026-07-29 query-spec typecheck pattern: `QueryContext.adminApi` is generic, but Vitest 4's `Mock<T>` normalizes generic return types to `unknown`, so even `vi.fn(async <T>() => value as T)` is not assignable. Put a correctly typed generic function on a typed owner, `vi.spyOn` that property, and return the property so static generic typing and runtime call tracking are both preserved. When a query mock's `mock.calls` arguments are inspected, declare its SQL parameter explicitly; otherwise `vi.fn(async () => ...)` infers an empty argument tuple and rejects index `0`.
- 2026-07-27: Standalone `tsc -p libs/features/invocation-ui/tsconfig.spec.json --noEmit` currently fails on pre-existing `globalThis` index-signature errors throughout `admin-api-hooks/src/lib/batchHooks.ts`; use the owning Nx typecheck as the authoritative project check and report the standalone dependency errors separately.
- 2026-07-27: After adding a regression test in a combined patch, verify the file exists before trusting a focused Vitest run; Vitest may exit successfully under `passWithNoTests` even when the intended test file was never created.
- 2026-07-27: `Status.tsx` must use an invocation's existing `last_failure` as the retry-popover fallback when no separately fetched transient error exists. The transient-error hook is deliberately disabled when `last_failure` is present; selecting only `transientError` for every retry makes the visible attempt chip disabled despite complete inline error data.
- 2026-07-29: `InvocationId` hover cards can briefly wrap `Target` and `Status` onto two rows before settling to one because `InvocationPopoverContent` replaces a `min-w-72` loading body with wider async content inside an auto-width, right-edge popover. React Aria initially retains the small overlay's positioned geometry; its overlay `ResizeObserver` then clears/recomputes positioning, allowing the `max-w-2xl` card to widen. Treat this as a dynamic-content/overlay measurement reflow, not intentional responsive wrapping or a font problem.
- 2026-07-29: Merge Virtual Object identity rows through one discriminated `addInstances(..., source)` helper. State, pending-work, and backlog are the meaningful merge semantics; VQueue metadata and invocation status are both pending-work sources. Keep lock-holder enrichment separate because it only updates selected identities and consumes a different row shape.
- 2026-07-29 status mapping naming: Once VQueue stage/status semantics live in the shared canonical definition, call the query-local physical mapping `SYS_INVOCATION_STATUS_DEFINITIONS`, not the overly broad `STORAGE_STATUS_DEFINITIONS`.
- 2026-07-29 invocation column order: Keep optional `vqueue_id` at the end of the Invocations table's canonical data-column order, immediately before the separately appended Actions column.
- 2026-07-29 shared VQueue identity column: `vqueue_id` belongs in `InvocationTableColumnKey` and the shared invocation-table renderer because both the Invocations table and VO inbox display invocation VQueue identity. Feature-gate it on `vqueues`, keep it optional in the Invocations Columns menu, and let the VO inbox reuse the shared config/cell rather than maintaining a one-off union and renderer.
- 2026-07-29 VO sidebar persistence: The current VO detail sub-item must remain visible after navigating to another section, matching invocation-detail memory. Prefer the active URL identity while on a VO detail, then fall back to an environment/base-path-scoped in-memory recent identity; only the active highlight should disappear. Retaining the item in DOM is insufficient because adaptive sidebar sizing collapses inactive sections—default the VO section open while a recent item exists, while preserving an explicit manual-collapse override.
- 2026-07-29 scope marker preference: Use a small, readable neutral `SCOPE` text badge before the scope value in VO identity chips. A stock Focus icon is visually clean but does not explain the field, while compressing `SCOPE` inside focus brackets makes the text illegible.
- 2026-07-29 pnpm diagnostic quirk: `pnpm list` can fail with `ERR_SQLITE_ERROR unable to open database file` against the Corepack cache even after a successful install/update. Verify an installed package directly from its `node_modules/<package>/package.json` when the dependency already typechecks and the manifest/lockfile agree.
- 2026-07-29 scope wordmark readability correction: A 24×16px focus badge with ~5–6px rendered `SCOPE` text remained unreadable, and the first 32×16px revision was still too subtle at ~9px. Use a 36×16px badge with roughly 11px rendered lettering and extra-bold weight; preserve chip height and accept the small horizontal increase.
- 2026-07-29 self correction on status normalization: Adding `stage` to `InvocationTableRow` is insufficient unless non-invocation VO entries also pass `entry.stage` into the synthetic row. Raw `new` cannot normalize to `Pending` without its `inbox` stage; visually verify the final row rather than assuming the shared mapper is receiving both fields.
- 2026-07-29 VO non-invocation status rule: Do not title-case raw VQueue entry states in `InvocationTableStatus`. Resolve them through the canonical invocation mapping and reuse the invocation badge presentation: `inbox/new → Pending`, `inbox/started → Ready`; retain the generic badge only for unknown states.
- 2026-07-29 browser wrapper correction: Browser-client locators and the page wrapper expose no `.hover()` method, and `playwright.evaluate` runs in a restricted DOM facade without event constructors. Do not assume raw Playwright parity; verify hover tooltip structure/code unless a documented CUA pointer operation is available.
- 2026-07-29 VO sidebar truncation: The active Virtual Object detail sub-item must expose its complete `service / key / scope` identity in a hover tooltip; segmented truncation alone is not sufficient for long service names.
- 2026-07-29 VO sidebar detail pattern: When a Virtual Object instance is open, surface its full `service / key / scope` identity as the active `extraSubItem` directly beneath Virtual Objects, mirroring invocation-detail navigation. Match scope as well as pathname so two scoped instances sharing service/key are not conflated.
- 2026-07-29 scope icon preference: Use a custom focus-brackets glyph with the word `SCOPE` inside for scope identity; do not use the generic `ScanBox` glyph in the Virtual Object scope segment.
- 2026-07-29 scope-chip visual correction: In VO instance rows, a bare focus icon plus `vo-b` does not communicate scope, but framing the full word `SCOPE` inside the focus glyph looked like a badge inside a badge and was reverted. Keep `Focus` and test a lightweight textual prefix inside the chip—prefer muted `scope:` followed by the normal-emphasis value (`scope: vo-b`)—consistently in table, hero, and breadcrumb. Do not rely on color or tooltip for the primary semantics.
- 2026-07-29 VO hero polish: The desktop lock summary should be one compact status-tinted capsule, not loose text. Structure it as `Lock holder | [clickable identity] | Held for [duration]`, with subtle dividers, a lightly raised holder surface, matched 32px height/rounded geometry, and no presence below `lg`.
- 2026-07-29 responsive lock summary: Hide the entire VO hero “Lock held by…” summary below `lg`; do not progressively reduce it to icon/ID fragments. The lock-holder table row remains the small-screen representation.
- 2026-07-29 VO lock presentation: Keep the lock holder as the first operational table row, with “Lock held for…” appended in its Status cell. Also summarize the persistent relationship in the hero's unused right side as “Lock held by [invocation] for [duration]”; keep handler/retry/status details in the table, make the invocation clickable, and leave the right side empty when unlocked.
- 2026-07-29 self correction: Before a multi-file patch, match the exact existing import formatting; the first VO lock-summary patch expected a multiline import where the file used one line and therefore applied nothing. Split broad UI patches into smaller verified edits when context is fragile.
- 2026-07-29 legacy lock semantics: Without VQueues, `sys_keyed_service_status` exposes only the holder `invocation_id`; it has no acquisition timestamp. For an exclusive Virtual Object holder, derive the UI's lock start from the already-hydrated invocation's `running_at`: legacy lock assignment and the first Running transition occur in the same state-machine event. Treat it as derived and omit it if `running_at` is unavailable.
- 2026-07-29 copy preference: For the truncated Virtual Object inbox footer, say “Showing 25 inbox entries.” Do not describe them as the “first” entries or as ordered by “scheduling time.”
- 2026-07-29 self correction: When consolidating concurrently started inbox queries into one `Promise.all`, preserve their established start order (`lock`, entries, count). Promise-array expressions execute left-to-right, and changing the order created noisy query-sequence snapshot failures even though results were equivalent.
- 2026-07-29 architecture correction: Do not decorate a completed `Response` with an independently resolved inbox-count promise. Resolve inbox count alongside entries and lock details in the existing orchestration, pass the resolved number through response options, and include it when `buildInboxResponse` creates the response body.
- 2026-07-29 limit rule hierarchy: For scope `acme` and limit key `team/user-42`, the invocation consumes the L1 `team` counter as well as the L2 `team/user-42` counter, so an `acme/team` L1 rule constrains it and any other `team/*` descendants. An L2 rule can constrain it additionally. A bare `team` rule pattern names scope `team`, not L1 `team`, because rule patterns begin with scope.
- 2026-07-29 limit-key display rule: Show `l1` for a level-1 counter and the full `l1/l2` path for a level-2 counter; never show bare `l2`, because it is only meaningful beneath its parent. For invocation data, show the full supplied `limit_key` string. For a scope-only counter, show no limit key (or an explicit “Scope only” label where needed).
- 2026-07-29 custom limit-key icon direction: The user proposed the supplied rounded Y/split glyph rotated 90° clockwise. This gives an LTR scope-to-subdivided-buckets reading and is a promising simpler alternative to the stepped three-node glyph; keep `ListTree` as the temporary stock icon until a custom icon is adopted.
- 2026-07-29 limits icon selection: Use Lucide `ListTree` for limit key for now. A possible future custom `LimitKey` glyph should show one nested scope-to-level-1-to-level-2 path (`○`, `└─○`, `  └─○`), not a generic branching tree, security key, or route.
- 2026-07-29 Restate limits semantics: A limit key is an invocation's optional one- or two-level concurrency classification within a required scope (for example `team/user`). It is not resource identity, authentication, or a rule. The invocation consumes capacity from the scope and every supplied key level simultaneously, while rules assign caps to matching levels. For iconography, emphasize hierarchical subdivision/nested concurrency buckets.
- 2026-07-29 limits icon decision: Use Lucide `SlidersHorizontal` for a rule. The user rejected `KeyRound`, `Route`, and `Fingerprint` for limit key, so avoid security-key, path, and identity metaphors when proposing that icon.
- 2026-07-29 icon preference: For limits UI, `Gauge` is accepted for a live counter, and scope should use `Focus` or a focus-square treatment. Treat a limit key as the counter's identity and a rule as its matching pattern; generic key and rule-book icons do not communicate those meanings well.
- 2026-07-29 correction: For `sys_rules` icon language, distinguish a counter (the counter referenced by a rule) from a numeric count. Do not suggest `Hash` for it, and prefer icons grounded in the existing rule/counter domain model.
- 2026-07-29: The Virtual Object instance hero should use the shared `InvocationStatusHeader`, with its tone driven by the current lock holder and the default neutral treatment when no lock exists. Match invocation-header geometry exactly: 56px outer height, 30px target chip, and 8px chip radius at the tested desktop width; do not duplicate the header shell or force the VO target to `h-9`.
- 2026-07-29 correction: Keep the Virtual Object target's first segment at 6px left padding in both the hero and instances table. Put the padding in shared `VirtualObjectInstanceTarget` instead of duplicating descendant overrides at each call site.
- 2026-07-29: Apply `mix-blend-luminosity` to the VO hero target, as the invocation hero does, so neutral chip whites and grays inherit the status-tinted header color. Keep this treatment local to the hero.
- 2026-07-29: The VO detail page must set the same ContentPanel sticky-header reservation as the invocation page (`--cp-toolbar-top:5rem` and `--cp-toolbar-tuck:5rem`). Without it, the tabs stick at the viewport top underneath the floating hero instead of reserving space below it.
- 2026-07-29: Remove the VO Service card once its navigation is replaced in the hero identity chip. Keep the breadcrumb strictly for back-navigation; make only the hero's service segment open the existing service details panel, while key and optional scope remain inert identity segments.
- 2026-07-29 final correction: Use separate angled `Chip` components for the VO hero when a real visible gap is required; a shared shell always paints behind internal segment spacing. Keep the group-level status blend, white simulated borders, no per-chip blurred shadow, and wrap the service chip in its link so hover does not replace the blended background.
- 2026-07-29 correction: Use 28px height and a 12px `--chip-radius` for the separate VO hero chips, and retain `Chip`'s shape-following shadow. Keep the inward-facing edges angled and leave the shared table target at its default geometry.
- 2026-07-29 root cause: On an angled `Chip`, `clip-path` overrides the root's normal border-radius, so the filter shadow followed square straight-side corners behind a rounded inner surface. Encode the straight outer radius directly into the `shape()` paths for left-angled and right-angled chips. For the VO hero, inset the segment surface by 2px and use `calc(var(--chip-radius) - 2px)` for its straight-side inner radius so the white frame and radii match.
- 2026-07-29 root cause: Even with the corrected rounded silhouette, `TruncateWithTooltip` clipped the chip shadow on the top, left, and bottom through two nested `overflow-hidden` truncation spans. Give it an opt-in `overflowVisible` mode for composite controls whose own inner text elements already handle truncation; enable that mode on `VirtualObjectInstanceTarget`.
- 2026-07-29 correction: Do not apply straight-side inner radii to every first/last segment in separate angled chips; that makes gray/blue surfaces into rounded rectangles inside angled shells. Model the surface inset as `--chip-inset`: use it for vertical/straight-side margins and `calc(--chip-radius - --chip-inset)` only on straight outer sides, while angled sides remain clipped by and fill their angled shell.
- 2026-07-29 temporary visual surface: `/ui/chip-test` renders the exact two- and three-chip VO header targets at 3× scale on the status background. Keep it while this design is under review, then remove `apps/web-ui/app/routes/chip-test.tsx` and its route entry after sign-off.
- 2026-07-29 final abstraction: Put the repeated identity-header geometry in `ChipGroup variant="header"`: 28px height, 12px outer radius, 2px surface inset, white shape border, visible shadow, and group-level luminosity blending. Header consumers choose only chip content and `straight`/`angled` edges. Document the composition in `libs/ui/chip/README.md` instead of reconstructing the CSS on each page.
- 2026-07-29 depth palette: Keep nested identity segments neutral rather than semantic: service `white`, key `zinc-50`, scope `zinc-100`, with progressively darker zinc text/icons. The header group's luminosity blend adapts those grays to the current status-tinted background; scope must not introduce a fixed blue hue.
- 2026-07-29 implementation detail: A reusable chip-group variant must set `--chip-radius` and `--chip-inset` on each `[data-chip]` wrapper, not only `[data-chip-root]`. The wrapper owns the shape clip path and its filter shadow, while the root owns height and typography; styling only the root silently gives the outer silhouette the default radius.
- 2026-07-29 tool correction: After stopping or restarting a visual-test dev server, verify the exact listener with `lsof`; a targeted `kill` may leave an existing listener alive, causing Nx/Vite to fall back to another port and its HMR socket to conflict even though the rendered page remains usable.
- 2026-07-29 correction: For the multi-chip VO hero, remove each angled chip's blurred drop shadow and apply `mix-blend-luminosity` to the containing group, not the individual chip roots—the angled-edge filter creates a stacking context that otherwise prevents their backgrounds from inheriting the status tint. Use Box for service, VirtualObject for key/instance, and the shared ScanBox icon for scope.
- 2026-07-29 correction: Removing only `--chip-shadow` does not remove the gray protrusions around independently angled chips; `Chip` also simulates its angled border with four directional drop shadows. Make `--chip-border-color` transparent in the status-tinted hero. Wrap the service `Chip` in the service `Link` instead of using `Chip.href`, because the chip's internal hover pseudo-element changes the blended background.
- 2026-07-29 correction: In the final shared-shell VO target, use `bg-zinc-50` for the key/instance segment, matching the invocation target. With `mix-blend-luminosity` on the continuous chip, it picks up a subtle version of the status-header hue instead of reading as flat gray or pure white.
- 2026-07-29 correction: To make the narrow VO segment gap perceptible, the shared `Chip` root must be transparent and every segment must paint its own background. Otherwise the gap merely exposes the root's white background and is indistinguishable from the adjacent white segment.
- 2026-07-29 browser correction: Once the browser binding and its documentation are already available in the persistent session, reuse them without rereading the Browser skill on a later turn.
- 2026-07-29: Render the VO State tab's “Edit state” action as a compact ContentPanel toolbar control, using the same toolbar alignment and compact secondary-button sizing as the Invocations table controls; do not present it as a large standalone raised button.
- 2026-07-29: Label the VO State toolbar action “Edit,” use a 12px pencil icon, and keep 4px bottom padding between the toolbar control and the state table.
- 2026-07-29: Make the VO State toolbar action a compact, always-visible split button. Keep Edit as the primary action, list both “Edit state…” and destructive “Delete state…” in the menu, and open the existing edit-state dialog with `isDeleting: true` for full-state deletion so confirmation, warnings, mutation handling, and query invalidation remain shared.
- 2026-07-29 correction: The shared full-state dialog must not say the new state will replace the previous state when `isDeleting` is true. State explicitly that all state for the key will be deleted; retain the replacement warning only for full-state edits.
- 2026-07-29 visual correction: In the VO State toolbar split button, offset only the primary Edit half 1px to the right so its seam meets the dropdown half cleanly without moving the dropdown or the overall toolbar alignment.
- 2026-07-29: Show the exact VQueue inbox population in the “Current & inbox” tab. Add `inboxCount` to the existing Virtual Object inbox response and derive it from `SUM(sys_vqueue_meta.num_inbox)` for the exact service/key/scope identity, so scoped objects include every limit-key VQueue without another client request. The count excludes the current lock holder unless that entry is itself still in the inbox.
- 2026-07-29 naming correction: Call the exclusive Virtual Object activity tab “Lock / inbox” and use “Lock and inbox” in screen-reader labels and empty/unavailable copy. The view contains the current lock holder plus queued inbox entries; “Current” is too vague.
- 2026-07-29 legacy count correction: When VQueues are unavailable, populate the same `inboxCount` response field with an exact `COUNT(*)` over `sys_inbox` filtered by service name and service key. Run it alongside the limited legacy inbox row read; do not hide the tab count merely because more than 25 legacy entries exist.
- 2026-07-29 correction: Keep the VO detail page object-centric. Useful additions are total inbox, VQueue count/drill-down, and exact lock age; scheduler flow/blocking diagnostics and concurrency-limit usage do not belong in the object-level summary. Put VQueue internals in a dedicated VQueues tab/detail surface, and keep limit key there only as queue identity—not as a limits dashboard. Never present `run_at` as a global cross-VQueue execution plan.
- 2026-07-29 browser correction: Do not assume a top-level variable declared inside a conditional browser-evaluation block will remain addressable in a later call; persist values outside the conditional or use a fresh measurement in the later call.
- 2026-07-29 browser correction: For this app's document-level scrolling, call `window.scrollTo(...)` in the restricted page evaluator; assigning `document.scrollingElement.scrollTop` can fail because the exposed object has a getter-only property.
- 2026-07-29 tool correction: Avoid unquoted shell globs for optional test files in zsh; use `rg --files | rg ...` so a no-match does not abort the inspection command.
- 2026-07-29 tool correction: When an `apply_patch` updates multiple files, keep every hunk syntactically complete before the next `*** Update File` marker; an unfinished hunk makes the entire patch fail verification.
- 2026-07-29: Keep the pinned lock-holder ID aligned with inbox IDs by replacing the normal glyph inside the existing identity square with the lock glyph. Use an optional `iconName` override on shared `InvocationId` whose default remains unchanged; do not add a separate square or overlay badge.
- 2026-07-29: On the Virtual Object detail page, present the lock holder as the pinned first row of `Current & inbox`, followed by the `run_at`-ordered inbox. Keep it as a normal accessible table row, add an explicit lock marker, and tint the row with the same status family (blue/info, orange/warning, red/danger, green/success, zinc/default); do not render a detached global lock card.
- 2026-07-29 tool correction: Run validation and inspection as separate parallel commands; do not chain shell commands with `&&`.
- 2026-07-29: Sort the scoped Virtual Object inbox only by `run_at`; VQueue ID, sequence number, and entry ID tie-breakers imply an ordering across equal timestamps that the scheduler does not provide.
- 2026-07-29 correction: Do not conflate VQueue `first_runnable_at` with inbox `run_at`. `first_runnable_at` is computed once as `max(created_at, original_run_at)` and remains historical; `run_at` is the mutable current scheduling gate, rewritten for scheduler-backed retry or explicit rescheduling. Neither provides a global execution order across multiple VQueues, whose selection also depends on DRR fairness and resource/lock availability.
- 2026-07-29: Treat legacy `getInbox.ts` as incorrect for the general VQueue queue contract. It reads one arbitrary `sys_vqueue_meta` row, filters by scheduler `is_active`, adds a separately inferred `sys_invocation` head even though `num_running` already includes it, and ranks across every VQueue stage. VQueue object identity can span multiple limit-key queues; lock ownership comes from `sys_locks`, and only `stage = 'inbox'` has a meaningful per-VQueue order. Its edit-state `size > 0` use is advisory and can still miss paused/dormant queued work.
- 2026-07-29: The edit-state dialog's `useGetVirtualObjectQueue`/legacy `getInbox.ts` request is advisory only. It consumes only `queue.size > 0` to change the activity banner and partial-update warning; it does not gate Save, contribute state data, or enforce mutation ordering/version safety. The exact-state read supplies the partial mutation version, and Restate queues the mutation server-side.
- 2026-07-29: Name the selected-instance backlog enrichment helper `getBacklogsForInstances`; reserve `...Query` names for SQL builders so it is not confused with the service-wide backlog-sort query.
- 2026-07-29: Virtual Object backlog loading is deliberately two-phase on the default path: bounded identity discovery first, then one aggregate query for at most the 50 selected identities, parallel with lock enrichment. This avoids a service-wide backlog `GROUP BY` by default. Explicit backlog sorting already performs the service-wide aggregate and must skip the selected-instance backlog query.
- 2026-07-29: In `listVirtualObjectInstances.spec.ts`, assert the complete ordered SQL call array in every test, including error and truncation cases; do not use partial `toContain` checks for query behavior.
- 2026-07-29: Keep the Virtual Object instance-list response limited to fields consumed by its route: identity, backlog, lock holder, and truncation. State and unfinished-work queries remain identity-discovery sources, but their provenance is not public response data. Keep lock-holder enrichment separate; a lock row does not prove that the earlier pending-work discovery queries found the identity.
- 2026-07-29: Prefer source-first names for Virtual Object SQL builders (`virtualObjectIdentitiesFromVqueueMeta...Query`, `...FromInbox...Query`) and retain predicate qualifiers such as `WithUnfinishedEntries` or `WithNonInboxEntries`; do not use vague source-only names or call paused/suspended entries “active.”
- 2026-07-29: The browser's restricted page evaluator may not expose global `parseFloat`; use `Number(value.replace('px', ''))` for bounded computed-style measurements.
- 2026-07-29 correction: A stable/content-box popover width alone did not keep the invocation summary on one row. Live geometry showed 638px usable width versus 640.2px for natural-width `Target`, `Status`, and a 12px gap. Do not use `flex-1` on `Target`; it fills the remaining row and visibly stretches the target chip. Preserve `flex-none` and reduce only the horizontal gap to 8px (`gap-x-2 gap-y-3`), which fits the measured natural content while retaining 12px vertical separation when genuine wrapping occurs. Measure flex items before changing their growth behavior or nudging container widths.
- 2026-07-29: `InvocationId` hover cards can briefly wrap `Target` and `Status` onto two rows before settling to one because `InvocationPopoverContent` replaces a `min-w-72` loading body with wider async content inside an auto-width, right-edge popover. React Aria initially retains the small overlay's positioned geometry; its overlay `ResizeObserver` then clears/recomputes positioning. Fix this on the measured `PopoverContent` with a stable responsive content width (`box-content` with `min(42rem, 100vw - container padding)`), remove competing inner min/max widths, and retain `flex-wrap` only for genuinely narrow viewports. A border-box `42rem` popover is 2px narrower inside than the former `max-w-2xl` content wrapper and permanently wraps this near-threshold row.
- 2026-07-25: `ContentPanelTab.label` is reused for inline tabs and menu items, so a route-level truncation wrapper also truncates the More/mobile dropdown. Use a separate full `menuLabel` for dropdowns, keep the inline label bounded with `TruncateWithTooltip`, and override the More popover width only where wider labels are expected rather than widening every dropdown globally.
- 2026-07-25: Make clickable `PanelTable` rows through React Aria's `onRowAction` for client-side routing and add a pointer cursor; do not overlay an anchor across the row. This preserves keyboard activation and lets nested links, such as a lock-holder `InvocationId`, keep their own destination.
- 2026-07-25: The Virtual Object list's `Lock acquired` column is a VQueue-only capability because only `sys_locks` supplies `acquired_at`; hide the entire column when `features.has('vqueues')` is false instead of rendering a legacy column full of dashes.
- 2026-07-25: Any `partition_key` carried through the browser query layer must be projected as `CAST(partition_key AS VARCHAR) AS partition_key` and accepted only as an unsigned-decimal string; JSON numbers can round u64 values above 2^53 before JavaScript sees them.
- 2026-07-25: Render Virtual Object backlog with the compact Rules-style meter, scaled against the largest backlog in the visible response because a VO has no configured capacity denominator; use red for values at least half the visible maximum, amber for smaller positive values, and muted zeroes. Render invocation lock holders with the default `InvocationId`. Show `acquiredAt` through the shared snapshot-relative date cell when `sys_locks` provides it; legacy non-VQueue servers only expose the holder and correctly render no acquisition time.
- 2026-07-25: Do not assume partition-pruning `sys_locks` is beneficial. The lock table stores only currently held locks: acquisition writes one row, release deletes it, and no released sentinel/history remains. `partition_key IN (...)` creates point-read fanout and up to one physical scan per supplied key, so for a 50-row VO page prefer one sparse-table scan filtered by the selected `(lock_name, scope)` pairs. Retain internal partition keys only to prune the larger bounded `sys_vqueue_meta` backlog enrichment; do not select/group by them in backlog aggregates or carry them through the hot-sort path.
- 2026-07-25: Keep Virtual Object backlog sorting opt-in at the API boundary. The normal list must retain bounded identity discovery and enrich only its selected page; only an explicit descending backlog request may run the exact service-wide aggregate. `partition_key` is an internal value used only to prune the bounded `sys_vqueue_meta` enrichment and must not appear in the public response.
- 2026-07-25: Global Virtual Object sorting by backlog is not the same bounded enrichment problem as displaying backlog for 50 discovered identities. Exact `ORDER BY SUM(num_inbox)` must scan and group every qualifying `sys_vqueue_meta` row for the service; with scoped VOs, many VQueues can share one `(lock_name, scope)`, so a direct per-VQueue TopK is not exact. Use a broad exact aggregate only as an explicit heavy path, a bounded/partial heavy-hitter query with disclosed partial semantics, or preferably a server-maintained object-level counter. After selecting rows, fetch lock holders in one exact-pair scan of the sparse `sys_locks` table.
- 2026-07-25: For a bounded Virtual Object instance table, define backlog as the exclusive waiting population (`SUM(sys_vqueue_meta.num_inbox)` across every VQueue for `(service, key, scope)`), excluding the lock holder and running/suspended/paused entries. Enrich the selected 50 identities in two batched, join-free queries: use discovered `partition_key` values to prune only the larger VQueue metadata query, and scan sparse current locks once by exact `(lock_name, scope)` pairs. Never issue one lock/inbox API call per row. Hydrate holder entry/invocation details only on the detail page.
- 2026-07-25: Browser locators are methods on `tab.playwright`, not directly on `tab`; call `tab.playwright.getByText(...)`, `getByRole(...)`, etc.
- 2026-07-25: The Restate TypeScript reference lives under `skills/building-restate-services/references/ts/api-and-pitfalls.md`; do not omit the `building-restate-services` directory when resolving the skill-relative path.
- 2026-07-24: A filtered sampled invocation summary must cap its source population before applying user filters. Keep only predicates that define a physical population, such as VQueue stage/entry kind or completed/time-window bounds, inside the cap. Treat every filtered sample as partial without separately counting the source population; users can choose exact mode when certainty matters.
- 2026-07-24: When the Invocations summary is actually truncated, never expose raw sample-row counts in its tabs, stage legend, service tabs, or segment tooltips. Use the sampled population only as the denominator: omit the redundant 100% badge from All/All services and show approximate percentages everywhere else. If a sampled request finishes below its cap, retain exact counts because the response is not partial.
- 2026-07-24: Without VQueues, keep Overview2's selected-range summary exact, but make the Invocations-page summary sampled by default for responsiveness and keep its estimated/exact selector visible so users can opt into the full legacy count. This summary mode is independent from the table's Partial/Complete scan control.
- 2026-07-24: Keep Overview2 hero query and data shaping out of `overview2-route.tsx`, but do not turn `useCompletionChart.ts` into another monolith. Keep range selection and data-source queries in `useCompletionRange.ts`, time-window and bucket arithmetic in `completionBuckets.ts`, segment/label/link shaping in `completionChartModel.ts`, and use `useCompletionChart.ts` only to compose those pieces and navigation. The corresponding in-flight totals, segments, breakdown state, and links belong in `useInFlightChart.ts`. The route owns composition, cross-cutting server state, refs, navigation layout, and rendering.
- 2026-07-24: Overview and Invocations should share the persisted invocation breakdown mode (`invocations-count-mode`). Keep its type and local-storage helpers in `@restate/features/user-preference`, while `@restate/features/status-chart` owns the reusable selector UI; sampled mode shows approximate percentages, exact mode shows counts. In Overview, present the compact selector as the natural phrase “Showing [estimated/exact] breakdowns”; retain the compact value-only form for constrained Invocations legends and popovers.
- 2026-07-24: Keep the Overview time-range selector text-like at rest, with a small rounded background appearing on hover/focus and becoming stronger while pressed; this preserves its metric-context feel while clearly signaling interactivity.
- 2026-07-24: Use an up/down chevron for expandable Inbox breakdown rows in Overview and Invocations so popover expansion is visually distinct from the right chevrons used for navigation.
- 2026-07-24: In the Invocations stage bar, replace coarse aggregate stages with their available joined segments: Inbox in both All and Not completed focus, and Completed outcomes inside the Completed portion of All. Keep each coarse stage as the fallback while its breakdown is missing or loading; All must retain every other stage around either expanded group.
- 2026-07-24: Overview has two range models. With VQueues, the completion range is local to the right gauge: 1h and 24h use bounded finished breakdowns, Overall is unbounded, the left gauge stays Overall, and skipped completed migration defaults the right gauge to 1h. Without VQueues, restore one URL-backed selector beneath the server; it defaults to 1h and applies a server-side `created_at` range to the exact shared summary, so both gauges, the bottom total, service/deployment data, and navigation links describe the same population. Overall intentionally performs the full exact legacy scan. When the completion-history chart is enabled with VQueues, keep it fixed to 24 hourly buckets and hide the range selector; retain `keepPreviousData` so a rolling cache-key change can display the previous history while the next window loads. In VQueue gauge mode, show each range as a quiet eyebrow immediately above its gauge's primary value: static Overall text on the left and an inline text dropdown on the right. Use natural eyebrow copy `Past hour`, `Past 24 hours`, and `Overall`, while keeping dropdown choices compact as `1h`, `24h`, and `Overall`. Keep the time-range chevron darker than its context text and prefix sampled gauge-center percentages with `~` just like sampled legend values.
- 2026-07-22: `pnpm exec playwright test /tmp/<spec>.js` does not discover a temporary spec outside this repo's configured test roots, even when passed explicitly. For one-off visualization interaction checks, run a small Playwright script directly instead of invoking the configured test runner.
- 2026-07-22: The bundled Visualize renderer lives relative to the skill file at `skills/visualize/scripts/render.py`, not at the plugin version root `scripts/render.py`. Resolve skill-relative script paths from the directory containing `SKILL.md`.
- 2026-07-22: A Virtual Object instance page must use `(service, service key, scope)` as its exact identity. The existing scoped state read/edit flow already preserves that triple, but the legacy object queue endpoint exposes only head/size and the newer VQueue detail endpoint starts from a VQueue id. A page that shows the actual lock holder plus mixed exclusive history (invocations and `state-mutation` entries) needs an identity-addressed VQueue query; do not equate scheduler head with the entry whose `has_lock` is true.
- 2026-07-22: `pnpm run check-licenses` can fail inside the filesystem sandbox with `ERR_PNPM_MISSING_PACKAGE_INDEX_FILE` for the generated Buf package because the script spawns a nested `pnpm licenses` process that cannot read the package-store index. Re-run it outside the sandbox; the Dependabot remediation then passed all 463 production-package licenses.
- 2026-07-22: During the Dependabot remediation, the first-pass dependency files became staged before the second-pass parent upgrades, leaving `MM` states even though staging was not requested. Inspect `git diff --cached` after dependency installs and unstage only the known remediation paths so the final working-tree diff stays coherent.
- 2026-07-22: After resolving all 55 currently open GitHub Dependabot ranges, `pnpm audit` reported nine newer registry advisories not yet in that GitHub list: `file-type@20.5.0` (2), `protobufjs@7.5.6` (4), `tmp@0.2.6` (1), `@opentelemetry/core@1.26.0` (1), and `@opentelemetry/propagator-jaeger@1.26.0` (1). Re-run the independent audit during security remediation; the alert list can lag newer advisories.
- 2026-07-22: The Codex-bundled pnpm 11.9.0 enforces an extra lockfile supply-chain policy and refuses this repo's existing `@buf/restatedev_service-protocol.bufbuild_es@2.2.3-...` entry because it has no integrity field. Do not relax the policy or run its suggested clean rebuild for ordinary dependency updates; use the repo-pinned pnpm 11.0.8 under Node 24 instead.
- 2026-07-22: This environment's `gh api` rejects combining `--slurp` with `--jq`/`--template`. For paginated Dependabot enumeration, use `--paginate --jq '.[] | [...] | @tsv'` to emit compact rows, then aggregate them separately.
- 2026-07-22: Dependabot alert 237 (`form-data` CRLF injection, GHSA-hmw2-7cc7-3qxx) is a valid version match on `form-data@4.0.5`, but the package is development-only and not shipped in the web app. The only concrete vulnerable call path found is Redocly Respect's multipart Arazzo request parser; this repo invokes only `redocly lint`, so ordinary app/CI behavior does not reach it. The existing pnpm override only raises `<4.0.4` to `>=4.0.4`; remediation should raise vulnerable 4.x to `>=4.0.6` and refresh the lockfile.
- 2026-07-22: Running/retrying needs dual semantics: keep the outer Running badge and activity dots blue, while retaining the original neutral/white attempt chip with orange text and warning icon and no “Retrying” prefix. Keep Backing-off fully orange because warning is its primary state.
- 2026-07-22: A running invocation is orange only when computed `isRetrying` is true; that currently requires `retry_count > 1` plus `last_failure`. VQueue `retry_count_since_last_stored_command` resets to 0 on a new command and increments on retry-worthy errors, so VQueue running should use `> 0` without `last_failure`; keep the last-failure guard for legacy `sys_invocation.retry_count`, which counts starts since the current leader began and can outlive command progress. `Status.tsx` currently fetches the transient error only after `isRetrying` is true, so using `last_failure` for the VQueue decision creates a circular gate.
- 2026-07-22: The admin API generator deliberately runs `redocly lint ... || true`; direct Redocly lint currently reports 196 pre-existing OpenAPI compatibility errors. Treat that lint as informational unless the changed schema adds a new diagnostic.
- 2026-07-22: `NX_DAEMON=false pnpm nx typecheck query` and `NX_DAEMON=false pnpm nx typecheck web-ui` can hang for about two minutes and fail with `NX Failed to start plugin worker` without reaching TypeScript. Retry important Nx checks outside the process sandbox; for query-library verification, run its Vitest config and fall back to `pnpm exec tsc -p libs/data-access/query/tsconfig.lib.json --noEmit`.
- 2026-07-22: For VQueue-backed invocation attempts, populate public `retry_count` from `sys_vqueue_entry_status.retry_count_since_last_stored_command`; `retry_attempts` is a different scheduler metric and can severely undercount the attempt ordinal shown in `Status.tsx`.
- 2026-07-16: Scope filters need `IS NULL` / `IS NOT NULL` in addition to string comparisons. Keep `EQUALS` first for the persistent empty Scope clause so opening the state route does not silently default to unscoped objects.
- 2026-07-16: Scoped Virtual Objects require both `/version` features `vqueues` and `scoped_virtual_objects` (underscore; the server config option uses hyphens). State listings may span scopes when enabled, but exact reads for an object with no scope must still add `scope IS NULL` so an unscoped key never merges with the same key in scoped namespaces.
- 2026-07-16: A real scoped-VO `state` export confirms identity collisions are expected: `myObject3/i1` appears under scopes `a`, `c`, and NULL, and `myObject3/i2` under `a` and `b`. Treat `(service_name, service_key, scope)` as the state-object identity everywhere; missing JSON `scope` represents SQL NULL.
- 2026-07-16: Resolve the `building-restate-services` skill's relative references from its own directory (`skills/building-restate-services/references/...`), not from the plugin's top-level `skills/` directory.
- 2026-07-16: Avoid embedding escaped single quotes inside a single-quoted zsh `git log -S` argument; use a safely double-quoted fixed string instead.
- 2026-07-16: `gh api ... -f ref=...` defaults to POST unless `-X GET` is supplied; include `-X GET` when reading repository contents with query fields.
- 2026-07-16: `convertFilters` quotes string field identifiers, so SQL tests must expect `"scope" IS NULL` / `"scope" = ...`, not unquoted `scope`.
- 2026-07-16: The local shell currently runs Node 22 while this repo requires Node >=24; Nx targets can exit after only the engine warning, and standalone state-route `tsc` then sees stale React types without `useEffectEvent`. Use a Node 24 runtime before treating those checks as authoritative.
- 2026-07-16: The `admin-api-spec:create` target ends with `nx format`, which staged the files it formatted in this environment. Inspect the index after generation and unstage those files unless the user asked for staging.

## Corrections

| Date       | Source | What Went Wrong                                                                                                                                                                       | What To Do Instead                                                                                                                                                              |
| ---------- | ------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 2026-07-29 | user | Inferred pending-work provenance from a later lock-holder enrichment and kept unused `hasState`, `hasPendingWork`, and `limit` fields in a new API response. | Treat discovery sources and lock enrichment as separate facts. Before shipping a new endpoint, audit its only runtime consumer and expose only identity, displayed metrics/details, and truncation. |
| 2026-07-25 | user | Showed a `Lock acquired` column on legacy servers even though their lock source has no acquisition timestamp, leaving a column of dashes. | Gate the complete column on `features.has('vqueues')`; only VQueue-backed `sys_locks` responses can populate it. |
| 2026-07-25 | user | Converted query-result `partition_key` values with `String(value)`, which cannot recover a u64 already rounded during JSON decoding, and treated every backlog aggregate row as evidence of pending work even when its sum was zero. | Cast partition keys to VARCHAR in every discovery projection and reject non-string/non-decimal values at merge time. Set `foundInPendingWork` from backlog enrichment only when backlog is greater than zero. |
| 2026-07-25 | self | Replaced an SQL snapshot through a JavaScript tool string and under-escaped backslashes, so the percent and underscore escapes disappeared in the test literal. | Use a raw patch payload for SQL escape sequences, then run the focused snapshot test immediately. |
| 2026-07-24 | user | Counted the bounded source separately so filtered samples could sometimes be labelled exact, which introduced extra CTEs and filter/no-filter query branches. | For any sample with applied filters, conservatively assume the cap was hit and mark it partial; keep one sampled query shape with filters outside the source limit. |
| 2026-07-24 | user | Added several fragment-level SQL assertions for sampled summary regressions, making the full generated query harder to review. | Assert generated SQL as a whole inline snapshot in query tests; keep response semantics in separate assertions. |

Save follows the SELECTED mode, not content-guessing (no base64/text ambiguity). Custom-codec caveat: base64 mode bypasses `CodecRuntimeProvider` encoders for that key (binary-state + custom-codec was already broken — not a regression). E2E proof (binaryStore/fallback-demo fixture, blob=PNG-header bytes + JSON note): per-key binary save → byte-array body, stored byte-exact; invalid base64 → error, no request; Replace form → blob row defaults Binary, note row defaults Text, save keeps blob byte-exact + minifies note. ## User Preferences

- For overview content panel polish, do not change shared `ContentPanel`/`ContentPanelHeader` chrome. Keep chrome stable and scope compactness to the controls inside the overview header.
- Uses Tailwind CSS with `tv()` from `@restate/util/styles` for component variants
- Never use string interpolation for dynamic Tailwind classes — always use `tv()` with variants
- Never add comments unless explicitly requested
- Nothing should wrap in entry rows — if not enough space, elements should shrink
- When changing technical direction mid-task, be explicit about what failed, why, and get approval for the new approach before writing code
- Use `--theme(--color-*/opacity%)` for colors in Tailwind arbitrary values, not `rgba()` or `color-mix()`
- When debugging data issues, add console.log early rather than theorizing for many rounds
- When debugging UI/CSS bugs, DIAGNOSE FIRST — ask user to inspect DevTools, identify the element, check computed styles — then fix. Never throw speculative fixes at the wall
- Wait for user instruction before making changes — don't guess and iterate when told to wait
- Don't modify shared UI library components when the fix belongs in the consuming page

## Patterns That Work

- VQueue V2 list population routing can avoid every `sys_vqueues`/`sys_invocation_status` join: direct single-table candidates when one source suffices; a one-million-ID metadata semi-join plus `LIMIT N+1` sentinel for service-filtered VQueue statuses; ordered `sys_invocation_status` pages plus bounded `sys_vqueue_entry_status` point refinement for other invocation filters; and parallel merge only for shared `created_at`. Keep explicit stage pruning in every VQueue scan.

- For TanStack persisted queries with `staleTime: Infinity`, always set the persister option `refetchOnRestore: 'always'`. Treat persisted data as a warm placeholder, not final truth; otherwise localStorage can keep old server data forever.
- For ECharts custom series with grouped child shapes, use `$mergeChildren: 'byName'` plus child `name`s when one child has live/pulse behavior and siblings are stable; put transition lists on the nested `shape` (`transition: ['y', 'height']`) when only vertical bar growth should animate, so x/width changes from polling or axis reflow do not slide old bars horizontally.
- For the overview hero completion chart, the flanking legends should start at the same container breakpoint as the desktop chart (`@min-[64rem]/hero`), using five grid columns with constrained `minmax` side tracks so labels truncate instead of hiding the legends on short wide screens.
- For shared ECharts interactions, keep `instance.on/off` lifecycle and visual hover syncing inside `@restate/ui/charts`; feature charts should expose behavior-level actions on the visual primitive (`<Slice onSelect>`, `<Series onSelect>`) rather than chart-level event params.
- When smooth-interpolating timestamps between API polls, advance related timestamps (e.g., `dataUpdatedAt`) by the same elapsed amount to avoid flicker — simplest form: use `Date.now()` in live-follow mode
- For `Now` marker visibility under headroom smoothing, use a stable bound check (`dataUpdatedAt <= end (+epsilon)`) instead of a near-zero threshold that toggles every poll
- For right-edge `Now` cut-off, keep marker mounted and switch badge side by viewport half (render badge to the left when `Now` is in right half)
- If row dashes paint over `Now`, raise the `UnitsPortalTarget` z-layer in `JournalV2` (portal stacking), not just `z-index` inside `Units.tsx`
- Don't hide UI elements (like "Now" indicator) wholesale in a mode — they may serve a real purpose in edge cases (e.g., future-scheduled traces). Fix the root cause of flicker instead.
- For dying ticks cleanup, use inline `setTimeout` with key-guarded `setDyingTicksInfo` instead of a separate `useEffect` — avoids a reactive cleanup pattern for a one-shot timer
- For live-follow tick interval changes, pre-schedule the switch ~300-700ms before the next expected poll (estimated from `dataUpdatedAt` cadence) and render old ticks as a short `animate-out fade-out` overlay so the poll-time coordinate jump does not coincide with interval churn
- For admin-api hooks that reshape data, export a shared query-options helper and keep the selector in the shared `queryFn` so `useQuery` and `queryClient` callers get the same normalized data
- Keep exported query-options helpers focused on reusable config only; hook-specific API-status gating like `useAPIStatus()` should stay in the hook, not become a required helper argument

## Patterns That Don't Work

- Removing `grow`/`flex-auto` from data-entry div — makes content collapse instead of filling available space
- `w-full` inside a flex-wrap container creates circular width dependencies when ancestors are content-sized with `max-w-fit`
- `grow basis-20` on flex children forces them to expand beyond content width, stealing space from siblings
- Black rectangle tracing the arc shape (white alpha-trace on black) over the overview gauge in **Safari**, appearing on **hover** over a chart segment: it's an **SVG `<filter>` corruption**, and the chart was secretly SVG, not canvas. `echartsSetup.ts` registered ONLY `SVGRenderer` while `useEchartsInit` asked for `renderer: 'canvas'` → zrender silently falls back to the only registered painter (`node_modules/.../zrender/lib/zrender.js:56`, `rendererType = keys(painterCtors)[0]`) → EVERY chart rendered as SVG. ECharts SVG renderer emits slice `shadowBlur`/`shadowColor`/`shadowOffsetY` as an SVG `<filter><feDropShadow>` (`zrender/lib/svg/graphic.js:281,288`); on hover the emphasis re-renders that filtered arc and Safari corrupts the filter region into a black box. Fix: register `CanvasRenderer`, add a `renderer` prop to `<Chart>` (default `'svg'` to keep all other charts' current effective behavior), set StatusArc `renderer="canvas"` → shadow becomes a native canvas shadow, no SVG filter, all visuals preserved. DEAD ENDS that did NOT fix it (both reverted): Ferrofluid `filter: url(#goo)` rewrite; ECharts pie `emphasis: { disabled: true }`. Lesson: when a chart looks like a canvas-compositing bug, FIRST check which renderer is actually active — a registration/`init()` mismatch can silently force SVG, turning every `shadowBlur` into a Safari-fragile SVG filter.
- Don't write or read `ref.current` during render (e.g. `if (x) ref.current = x` in the component body) — violates React's purity rule (react.dev `useRef` Pitfall: the body must behave like a pure function). Update/read refs in **event handlers or effects**; if a value is genuinely needed during render, use `useState`. (Came up on the journal "remember last detailed selection" ref — I floated a render-time write as a "leaner alternative"; it's wrong. Correct impl updates the ref in the `setCompact`/`writeCategories` event handlers.)

## Domain Notes

- Internal `/query/*` routes do not have a separate router-level timeout. Handler downstream calls use ky timeouts from `QUERY_HANDLER_DOWNSTREAM_TIMEOUT_MS` (currently 10m): `this.query(...)` POSTs to `${baseUrl}/query`, and `this.adminApi(...)` uses the same budget; each downstream request gets its own timeout, while the original request `AbortSignal` is passed through.
- `SELECT 1 FROM sys_invocation_status LIMIT 1` is the app-wide query health check, not invocations-page data. `apps/web-ui/app/root.tsx` enables it on `RestateContextProvider`; `RestateContext.tsx` runs it while status is `HEALTHY` but currently has no fixed refetch interval, and `adminApi()` adds `refetchOnMount: true`.
- Introspection predefined query templates live in `libs/features/introspection-route/src/lib/predefinedQueries.ts`; each entry carries UI `description`, SQL text, hidden `performance` (`safe`/`caution`/`heavy`), and optional `requiredFeatures` such as `vqueues`.
- overview2 card skeletons must reserve the loaded row height or the card jumps on (re)fetch. `ServiceCard` pins its primary row via an always-rendered icon cell (`h-7 w-7` in its own grid column), so its short text skeleton (`h-6`) never shifts. `DeploymentCard`'s icon lives INSIDE the `Deployment` (primary), and the whole primary cell is swapped for the skeleton during `isDeploymentsFetching` — so the skeleton itself must reserve the loaded height (measured 34px = `h-7` icon + py + border; note `Deployment` primary's own `min-h-[2.625rem]` is overridden to `min-h-auto` here). Fix: skeleton = `flex min-h-[2.125rem] items-center gap-2` mirroring `[icon h-7 w-7][text bar]`. Always measure the loaded row height before sizing a card skeleton.
- `nx serve web-ui -c mock` does NOT serve the SQL `/query` endpoint — POST `/query` returns an HTML 404 ("Cannot POST /query"). So invocation-detail pages (which run SQL via client-side handlers in `libs/data-access/query`) can't load real data there, and a clean `UI0004` "invocation not found" can't be reproduced naturally (the handler's no-rows→UI0004 branch never runs; you get a generic error instead). Network-level Playwright route intercepts hit the raw SQL POST, BELOW the handler that emits UI0004, and the SQL query client parses errors differently from the admin `middleware.ts` (which does read `restate_code`). To visually verify a specific error variant, temporarily force the code in the component (e.g. `const code = 'UI0004'`) and revert. Overview/deployments DO work in mock (`/deployments` on :4001).
- invocation-route renders `InvocationUnavailable` (early return) whenever `error && !journalAndInvocationData` — for ANY load error, not just not-found. UI0004 → calm neutral variant (ScanSearch badge, ERROR_CODES summary+help, no banner); any other error → red TriangleAlert badge + full `ErrorBanner`. Don't reintroduce the old behavior where the full page chrome (empty sticky header card + Journal tab) renders with the error buried inside `JournalV2`.
- Reusable empty/not-found/error placeholder: `EmptyState` from `@restate/ui/empty-state` (`libs/ui/empty-state`). Centered, intent-aware (`neutral|danger|warning|info|success` → badge+icon tone), props `{ title, description?, icon?: IconName, intent?, className? }` + `children` slot (rendered in a `gap-4` actions column for chips/banners/buttons). `InvocationUnavailable` composes it. Prefer this over hand-rolling another centered placeholder; the older `NoDeploymentPlaceholder`/`CrashError` predate it and could be migrated (don't touch `NoDeploymentPlaceholder` — user asked to leave it). Its `badge` follows the app-wide icon-chip language (user confirmed): flat `bg-white` / faint *flat* tint (`bg-red-50/40`), thin border, `shadow-xs`, `drop-shadow-md` on the icon. Do NOT use gradients or layered/inset shadows on icon chips/badges — that reads too glossy next to the flat white deployment/service/breadcrumb icons. ICON FILL GOTCHA: Lucide line icons (Database, ScanSearch, Invocation, etc.) are stroke-based — applying `fill-*` to them fills the silhouette and COVERS internal stroke lines (e.g. Database loses the top disk's front arc → "missing a line"). Render line icons stroke-only (`text-X-400 drop-shadow-md`, no fill — that's how `StatePanel`/sidebar render the blue Database). So `EmptyState` neutral icon = `text-zinc-400` (no fill); only the colored/closed-shape intents (danger `TriangleAlert`) keep `fill-X-100 text-X-500`.
- Huge-value editor crash (2026-06-12): a ~30MB state value crashed the EditState dialog. Two formatting layers were responsible: (1) `MonacoEditor.tsx` ran `editor.action.formatDocument` on every mount (+ `formatOnPaste`/`formatOnType: true`) — re-indenting a multi-MB JSON generates millions of main-thread edits → freeze/OOM; (2) `EditState.tsx` pretty-printed with `JSON.stringify(..., null, 4)`, inflating size several-fold and creating millions of lines. Fix: `MAX_EDITOR_FORMAT_LENGTH = 1_000_000` exported from `@restate/ui/editor` (defined in `Editor.tsx`, NOT in `.client/` — the client module is undefined during SSR, and Editor.tsx→MonacoEditor is dynamic-import-only so the static back-import creates no cycle); MERGED (PR from few-fixes, 2026-06-12): MonacoEditor gates `isLargeValue = value.length > MAX` (local const `MAX_EDITOR_FORMAT_LENGTH = 10_000_000`, user-chosen, in `.client/MonacoEditor.tsx`) → formatOnPaste/Type off, mount-format skipped (plain `updateStyles()` instead), and the forced `el.style.width = max(contentWidth,300)ch` is skipped so large values wrap at the container width instead of ~2160px behind the container's horizontal scrollbar. EditState passes a `useMemo`'d compact `JSON.stringify(...)` for both whole-state and per-key (no pretty-printing — Monaco's mount format prettifies ≤MAX values, and prop changes recreate the editor so it always runs; memo holds because TanStack v5.90.5 memoizes `useQueries` combine output and decode queries are staleTime Infinity). REIMPLEMENTED Replace-UI redesign (2026-06-12, built+e2e-verified in restate-web-ui-4, ported to the `replace` branch here same day, reconciled with the payload-size fixes): `rawState` back in `useDecodeState`'s combine; per-key dialog shows `rawState[key]` verbatim (submit runs `minifyJson` — see below); MonacoEditor's `isFormattableJson` gate back (`shouldFormat = !isLargeValue && isFormattableJson(value)`); whole-state Replace renders the composed JSON doc ONLY when every raw value JSON-parses (`hasRawValues` memo with `isJsonText`, early-exit `.some()`), otherwise a PER-KEY FORM (user's pick — see Corrections): rows live in ONE gray scroll container (`max-h-[50vh] overflow-y-auto rounded-xl border bg-gray-100 p-2` + `gap-2`); each `StateFormRow` is a react-aria `Disclosure` styled as a WHITE raised card (`group rounded-lg border bg-white shadow-xs overflow-hidden`, matches the Services accordion look): header row = borderless mono key `FormFieldInput` (12px/`min-h-9`/`outline-hidden`) + chevron `Button slot="trigger"` (`group-expanded:rotate-180`, the `expanded` variant comes from the `tailwindcss-react-aria-components` plugin) + ghost trash; `DisclosurePanel` (`border-t bg-gray-50`) holds the `FormFieldCode` editor. `defaultExpanded={value.length <= STATE_VALUE_EXPAND_THRESHOLD (200)}` — small values open, large collapse. CRITICAL/verified: react-aria's `useDisclosure` collapses via `hidden="until-found"` and KEEPS children mounted, so a collapsed row's `FormFieldCode` hidden input persists → collapsed keys still submit (e2e-proved: saved with a 250-char key collapsed, value survived). "Add key" button sits OUTSIDE/below the scroller, appends counter-keyed empty rows; save submits `Record<string,string>` with `partial: false`. Each row mounts its own Monaco — key-heavy state = N editors (acceptable; lazy-mount only if it bites). Shared `MonacoEditor` (`@restate/ui/editor`) opts (apply to ALL its consumers — journal `Value.tsx` viewers + the state editors; the SQL/introspection editor uses a SEPARATE Monaco setup, untouched): `scrollbar.alwaysConsumeMouseWheel: false` so the wheel chains to the surrounding scroll container when the editor has nothing more to scroll (fixes wheel getting trapped over an editor inside the rows scroller — safe everywhere, standard scroll-chaining); `renderValidationDecorations: 'off'` so JSON "invalid" red squiggles don't show on raw/non-JSON values (syntax COLOURING stays). Squiggles only ever showed on EDITABLE editors anyway (Monaco default `renderValidationDecorations: 'editable'` → readonly journal viewers were already clean). All-JSON Replace stays literal (no byte-preservation — user rejected it as misleading). Submit behavior after the merge with the payload fixes: every submit path (per-key Update dialog, form rows, all-JSON doc via `stringifyValues`) runs valid-JSON values through `minifyJson` (compact `JSON.parse`→`stringify`), so Monaco's display formatting never reaches storage and the byte-array/string body stays small; raw (non-JSON) values fail the parse and pass through byte-exact verbatim. `FORCE_KEY_FORM = true` TEMP toggle still on — user is comparing the accordion form vs the JSON editor for all-JSON state; the open decision is whether the form becomes the always-default or only for raw/mixed state. Plus `FormFieldCode` hidden-input fix (see Corrections). E2E-verified (in -4) with Playwright against dev server :4300 (app served under /ui/; state page = /ui/state/<service>; row cells have hover-`invisible` edit buttons — hover the value button then force-click): per-key shows raw verbatim, no-edit save byte-stable for raw values, typed `goodbye raw: text` stored verbatim. NOTE: an EMPTY-bytes state value fails `isJsonText` so it routes to the per-key form, and the old display silently DROPPED empty-value keys (stringify drops undefined) — the gate now prevents that delete-by-omission. Test fixture: `../typescript-basics-v2/src/state_quirk_service.ts` (`pnpm quirk-state`, port 9097) — `stateQuirk` virtual object; `seed` writes `rawText`=`hello` bytes via a text/plain serde (+ quoted/JSON controls), shared `readRaw` returns every key's stored bytes verbatim for before/after comparison. `Value.tsx` (journal payloads) shares the same Editor and is covered by the format gates. Monaco internals (verified in node_modules): wrap+tokenization are killed only when `largeFileOptimizations` (default true) AND (doc >20MB `LARGE_FILE_SIZE_THRESHOLD` OR >300k lines) — decided permanently at model construction; separately monarch skips tokenizing any line ≥`maxTokenizationLineLength` (20k). So compact large values <20MB wrap fine (vertical space, plain text); >20MB renders as one line with the "Show more" chip.
- Vqueue status overlay (2026-06-12 audit): BOTH `listInvocations` (batch `fetchVqueueStatuses`, `WHERE entry_id IN (...)`) and `getInvocationJournalV2` (singular `fetchVqueueStatus`, `WHERE id=<vqueueId> AND entry_id=...` with hint+single-retry) already fetch `sys_vqueues` in parallel and feed `convertInvocation`→`applyVqueueStatus` (only rewrites `ready`+vqueue `backing-off` → overlays status/next_retry_at/retry_count/last_start_at). `getInvocation.ts` does NOT overlay (has TODO(vqueue), low priority — used by Restart/Resume dialogs for target only). LATENT BUG found, not yet fixed: #658 renamed `num_errors`→`num_attempts` in both SELECTs but only updated the singular mapper; `fetchVqueueStatuses` (vqueue.ts:80) still maps `num_errors: row.num_errors` (unprojected → undefined), so list-path `retry_count` is clobbered to undefined for backing-off rows — likely why #664 hid the attempt count in `Status.tsx`. Fix = map `num_attempts: row.num_attempts`. `sys_vqueues` is undocumented (0 hits in docs); entry_id-only lookups likely scan all queue entries while the id+entry_id form can prune to one queue — that's why the journal path threads the vqueueId hint.
- Entry layout in invocation-route: Entry row (flex) contains [number div (fixed)] [data-entry (max-w-fit flex-auto)] [dashed line (flex-auto, min-w-20)]
- Call component renders a Fragment — its children become direct children of data-entry
- The + button in Call is absolutely positioned relative to the Entry row
- Expression.tsx wraps content; inner div and span contain all inline elements
- InputOutput has `contents` display — its children participate directly in parent flex
- Target component has complex internal flex layout with `flex-auto` on the root
- `base-20` on Expression line 70 is likely a typo for `basis-20` (gets ignored by Tailwind)
- Journal compact/detailed view: `JournalV2` renders in 3 spots — the full timeline (`invocation.route.tsx`, the only one with the toggle) and two embedded `withTimeline={false}` previews (`JournalSection.tsx`, the `cells.tsx` row popover) that must stay compact. View state is the `?detail=` URL param (`useJournalDetail.ts`) = a set of categories {errors, completions, lifecycle}; compact = empty/absent param. ALWAYS gate URL-driven view state on `withTimeline` so the embedded previews never read/write it (`detail = withTimeline ? journalDetail.detail : COMPACT_DETAIL`). Those 3 categories map 1:1 to the rows `useProcessedJournal.shouldIncludeEntry` hides: transient errors (`Event: TransientError`), command-bound notification completions (`parentCommand && category==='notification'`), and past non-active `Suspended`/`Paused` events. `Entry.tsx`'s last-failure `findLast` only cares about the errors category (`detail.errors || !isTransientError`).
- Validation gotcha: `pnpm nx typecheck web-ui` reports pre-existing `.react-router/types/**` errors (`Generic type 'GetAnnotations' requires 1 type argument`) from react-router typegen, unrelated to any lib edit. To validate a change, typecheck the specific lib(s) instead — `pnpm nx typecheck <lib>` (uses `tsconfig.lib.json`, skips the generated app route types).

  | 2026-02-17 | self | Refactoring units into a portal wrapper dropped effective height/positioning, making interval labels disappear | Keep portal units wrapper `relative h-full` and render `Units` as `absolute inset-0` |
